v</annotation></semantics></math>, we have <math id="S3.Thmtheorem1.p1.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathrm{M}(u\cup v)=\min(\mathrm{M}(u),\mathrm{M}(v),w(uv))" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>∪</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>min</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathrm{M}(u\cup v)=\min(\mathrm{M}(u),\mathrm{M}(v),w(uv))</annotation></semantics></math>.
With this notation, we say that a merge of an edge <math id="S3.Thmtheorem1.p1.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="uv" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">uv</annotation></semantics></math> in <math id="S3.Thmtheorem1.p1.m15" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> is <math id="S3.Thmtheorem1.p1.m16" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)-" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>−</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)-</annotation></semantics></math><em id="S3.Thmtheorem1.p1.1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_upright">good</em> if and only if</span></p>
<table id="S3.Ex1" class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table">

<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math id="S3.Ex1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{\max(w_{\max}(u),w_{\max}(v))}{\min(\mathrm{M}(u),\mathrm{M}(v),w(uv))}\leq 1+\epsilon." display="block" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>max</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>min</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{\max(w_{\max}(u),w_{\max}(v))}{\min(\mathrm{M}(u),\mathrm{M}(v),w(uv))}\leq 1+\epsilon.</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S3.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p3.1" class="ltx_p">When <math id="S3.p3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ϵ</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> is clear from the context or irrelevant, we will sometimes say good instead of <math id="S3.p3.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good.
Observe that the value of <math id="S3.p3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathrm{M}(u)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathrm{M}(u)</annotation></semantics></math> only depends on the sequence of merges which created <math id="S3.p3.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math>.
In contrast, <math id="S3.p3.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{\max}(u)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{\max}(u)</annotation></semantics></math> is a function of both <math id="S3.p3.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> and some merges outside of <math id="S3.p3.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math>, as it also depends on the current set of neighbors of <math id="S3.p3.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math>.
This means that <math id="S3.p3.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathrm{M}(u)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathrm{M}(u)</annotation></semantics></math> is well defined given a particular merge tree.
However, to compute <math id="S3.p3.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{\max}(u)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{\max}(u)</annotation></semantics></math> we also need to specify which merges have been performed.</p>
</div>
<figure id="S3.F4" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="/html/2308.03578/assets/terahac-mvalues-figure.svg" id="S3.F4.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" width="476" height="191"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure">Figure 4. </span><span id="S3.F4.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Example showing the need for <math id="S3.F4.m17" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M(\cdot)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">⋅</mo><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M(\cdot)</annotation></semantics></math> values in Definition <a href="#S3.Thmtheorem1" title="Definition 0 (Good merge). ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>.
Green edges correspond to merges which are <math id="S3.F4.m18" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good, and red edges correspond to merges which are not <math id="S3.F4.m19" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good.
After merging <math id="S3.F4.m20" class="ltx_Math" alttext="ab" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">ab</annotation></semantics></math> (which is <math id="S3.F4.m21" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good) we obtain a vertex <math id="S3.F4.m22" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{a,b\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo maxsize="1.110em" minsize="1.110em">{</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo maxsize="1.110em" minsize="1.110em">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{a,b\}</annotation></semantics></math> such that <math id="S3.F4.m23" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M(\{a,b\})=1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mo maxsize="1.110em" minsize="1.110em">{</mo><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi></mrow><mo maxsize="1.110em" minsize="1.110em">}</mo></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M(\{a,b\})=1</annotation></semantics></math>.
Therefore, the merge of <math id="S3.F4.m24" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{a,b\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo maxsize="1.110em" minsize="1.110em">{</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo maxsize="1.110em" minsize="1.110em">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{a,b\}</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="S3.F4.m25" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>c</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math> in the resulting graph is <em id="S3.F4.3.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">not</em> <math id="S3.F4.m26" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good, since <math id="S3.F4.m27" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\max(1+\epsilon,(1+\epsilon)^{2})/\min(1,\infty,1+\epsilon)=(1+\epsilon)^{2}&gt;1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>max</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo>,</mo><msup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>min</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\max(1+\epsilon,(1+\epsilon)^{2})/\min(1,\infty,1+\epsilon)=(1+\epsilon)^{2}&gt;1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>.
Hence, the algorithm is forced to merge <math id="S3.F4.m28" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>c</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="S3.F4.m29" class="ltx_Math" alttext="d" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics></math>. It is easy to see that allowing a merge of <math id="S3.F4.m30" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{a,b\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo maxsize="1.110em" minsize="1.110em">{</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo maxsize="1.110em" minsize="1.110em">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{a,b\}</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="S3.F4.m31" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>c</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math> would create a dendrogram, which is not <math id="S3.F4.m32" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<div id="S3.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p4.1" class="ltx_p">We will show that applying <math id="S3.p4.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges leads to a <math id="S3.p4.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate dendrogram (see Lemma <a href="#S3.Thmtheorem6" title="Lemma 0. ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6</span></a>).
For <math id="S3.p4.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon=0</annotation></semantics></math> this generalizes the RAC algorithm, as in this case the <math id="S3.p4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M(u)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M(u)</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S3.p4.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M(v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M(v)</annotation></semantics></math> terms in the denominator become irrelevant (see Observation <a href="#Thmobservation1" title="Observation 1. ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>).
However, they are crucially important for <math id="S3.p4.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon&gt;0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon&gt;0</annotation></semantics></math>, as shown in Figure <a href="#S3.F4" title="Figure 4 ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p5" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p5.1" class="ltx_p">In the initial graph <math id="S3.p5.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{1}</annotation></semantics></math>, a merge is good iff we have that <math id="S3.p5.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\max(w_{\max}(u),w_{\max}(v))/w(uv)\leq 1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>max</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\max(w_{\max}(u),w_{\max}(v))/w(uv)\leq 1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>, regardless of the maximum weight of an edge in the graph.
In general, each <math id="S3.p5.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate is <math id="S3.p5.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good, but the converse is not necessarily true.
Nevertheless, we can show that performing a sequence of good merges produces a
<math id="S3.p5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate dendrogram.
Before we formally state and prove this property, we first show a few auxiliary lemmas.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.Thmtheorem2" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Lemma 0.</h6>
<div id="S3.Thmtheorem2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.Thmtheorem2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.Thmtheorem2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Let <math id="S3.Thmtheorem2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{1},\ldots,G_{n}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>G</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>n</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{1},\ldots,G_{n}</annotation></semantics></math> be a sequence of graphs, in which each graph is obtained from the previous one by performing an arbitrary merge.
Let <math id="S3.Thmtheorem2.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> be a vertex (cluster) which exists in <math id="S3.Thmtheorem2.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{l},\ldots,G_{r}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>l</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{l},\ldots,G_{r}</annotation></semantics></math>.
Let <math id="S3.Thmtheorem2.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{\max}^{i}(v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msubsup><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi><mi>i</mi></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{\max}^{i}(v)</annotation></semantics></math> be the value of <math id="S3.Thmtheorem2.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{\max}(v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{\max}(v)</annotation></semantics></math> in <math id="S3.Thmtheorem2.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>, where <math id="S3.Thmtheorem2.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="l\leq i\leq r" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">l\leq i\leq r</annotation></semantics></math>.
Then <math id="S3.Thmtheorem2.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{\max}^{l}(v)\geq w_{\max}^{l+1}(v)\geq\ldots\geq w_{\max}^{r}(v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><msubsup><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi><mi>l</mi></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mrow><msubsup><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>≥</mo><mrow><msubsup><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi><mi>r</mi></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{\max}^{l}(v)\geq w_{\max}^{l+1}(v)\geq\ldots\geq w_{\max}^{r}(v)</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S3.p6" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p6.1" class="ltx_p">Now, we prove a useful invariant, which is satisfied when performing good merges.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.Thmtheorem3" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Lemma 0.</h6>
<div id="S3.Thmtheorem3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.Thmtheorem3.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.Thmtheorem3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Let <math id="S3.Thmtheorem3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> be a graph obtained from <math id="S3.Thmtheorem3.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> by performing <math id="S3.Thmtheorem3.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges.
Then, for each vertex <math id="S3.Thmtheorem3.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> of <math id="S3.Thmtheorem3.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>, we have that <math id="S3.Thmtheorem3.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{\max}(v)/\mathrm{M}(v)\leq 1+\epsilon." display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{\max}(v)/\mathrm{M}(v)\leq 1+\epsilon.</annotation></semantics></math></span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S3.2" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S3.p7" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p7.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p7.1.1" class="ltx_text">We prove the claim by induction on the number of good merges made.
The base case (no merges) follows trivially from the fact that <math id="S3.p7.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathrm{M}(v)=\infty" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathrm{M}(v)=\infty</annotation></semantics></math> for each vertex <math id="S3.p7.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math>.
Now, consider that some number of good merges have been made, and the resulting graph is <math id="S3.p7.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{i+1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{i+1}</annotation></semantics></math>.
Fix a vertex <math id="S3.p7.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math>.
If the vertex <math id="S3.p7.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> also exists in <math id="S3.p7.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>, the property follows by <a href="#S3.Thmtheorem2" title="Lemma 0. ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Lemma</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>.
Otherwise, <math id="S3.p7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> is created by merging two vertices <math id="S3.p7.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S3.p7.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{2}</annotation></semantics></math> (<math id="S3.p7.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v=v_{1}\cup v_{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>∪</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v=v_{1}\cup v_{2}</annotation></semantics></math>) using a <math id="S3.p7.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merge.
We have</span></p>
<table id="S3.Ex2" class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table">

<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math id="S3.Ex2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{w_{\max}(v)}{\mathrm{M}(v)}\leq\frac{\max(w_{\max}(v_{1}),w_{\max}(v_{2}))}{\mathrm{M}(v)}\leq 1+\epsilon." display="block" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac><mo>≤</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>max</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{w_{\max}(v)}{\mathrm{M}(v)}\leq\frac{\max(w_{\max}(v_{1}),w_{\max}(v_{2}))}{\mathrm{M}(v)}\leq 1+\epsilon.</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p id="S3.p7.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p7.2.1" class="ltx_text">In the first inequality we used reducibility (<a href="#S2.Thmtheorem1" title="Definition 0 ((
            , 
          )). ‣ 2. Preliminaries ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Definition</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>), which implies <math id="S3.p7.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{\max}(v)\leq\max(w_{\max}(v_{1}),\allowbreak w_{\max}(v_{2}))" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mi>max</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{\max}(v)\leq\max(w_{\max}(v_{1}),\allowbreak w_{\max}(v_{2}))</annotation></semantics></math>.
The second inequality follows from the definition of a good merge.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="Thmobservation1" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_observation">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Observation 1.</h6>
<div id="Thmobservation1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmobservation1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmobservation1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Let <math id="Thmobservation1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> be obtained from <math id="Thmobservation1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> by performing <math id="Thmobservation1.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges.
Then, a merge <math id="Thmobservation1.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="uv" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">uv</annotation></semantics></math> in <math id="Thmobservation1.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> is <math id="Thmobservation1.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>-good if and only if <math id="Thmobservation1.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w(uv)=\max(w_{\max}(u),w_{\max}(v))" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>max</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w(uv)=\max(w_{\max}(u),w_{\max}(v))</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S3.p8" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S3.p8.1" class="ltx_p">The proof uses Lemma <a href="#S3.Thmtheorem3" title="Lemma 0. ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>;
we provide it in the Appendix.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.Thmtheorem4" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_definition">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Definition 0.</h6>
<div id="S3.Thmtheorem4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.Thmtheorem4.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.Thmtheorem4.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Let <math id="S3.Thmtheorem4.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> be a dendrogram and <math id="S3.Thmtheorem4.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math> be its corresponding merge tree.
Consider a sequence of merges <math id="S3.Thmtheorem4.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> which contains all merges of <math id="S3.Thmtheorem4.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math> and is consistent with <math id="S3.Thmtheorem4.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math>.
We say that this sequence is a <em id="S3.Thmtheorem4.p1.1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_upright">greedy merge sequence</em> of <math id="S3.Thmtheorem4.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> if its obtained as follows.
Start with an empty sequence.
At each step append a merge <math id="S3.Thmtheorem4.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> of maximum weight, chosen such that the new expanded sequence is consistent with <math id="S3.Thmtheorem4.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S3.p9" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p9.1" class="ltx_p">A greedy merge sequence is a <em id="S3.p9.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">c</em>anonical merge sequence, in the sense that it achieves the best approximation ratio (i.e. the maximum approximation ratio of a merge is minimized) out of all consistent orderings of merges of <math id="S3.p9.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p10" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p10.1" class="ltx_p">Let <math id="S3.p10.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> be a dendrogram and <math id="S3.p10.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math> be its corresponding merge tree.
Let <math id="S3.p10.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> be a sequence of merges consistent with <math id="S3.p10.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S3.p10.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> be the graph obtained after applying them.
We say that the <em id="S3.p10.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">error</em> of a merge <math id="S3.p10.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>m</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m</annotation></semantics></math> which merges <math id="S3.p10.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S3.p10.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> is the maximum weight of an edge in <math id="S3.p10.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> divided by <math id="S3.p10.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w(uv)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w(uv)</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.Thmtheorem5" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Lemma 0.</h6>
<div id="S3.Thmtheorem5.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.Thmtheorem5.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.Thmtheorem5.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Let <math id="S3.Thmtheorem5.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G=(V,E,w)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G=(V,E,w)</annotation></semantics></math> be a weighted graph, <math id="S3.Thmtheorem5.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> be a dendrogram of <math id="S3.Thmtheorem5.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S3.Thmtheorem5.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>n</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}</annotation></semantics></math> be its greedy merge sequence.
Let <math id="S3.Thmtheorem5.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> be the maximum error of a merge in the produced sequence.
Then, <math id="S3.Thmtheorem5.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> is <math id="S3.Thmtheorem5.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate and is not <math id="S3.Thmtheorem5.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon^{\prime})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>′</mo></msup></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon^{\prime})</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate for any <math id="S3.Thmtheorem5.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon^{\prime}&lt;\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon^{\prime}&lt;\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S3.3" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S3.p11" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p11.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p11.1.1" class="ltx_text">Let <math id="S3.p11.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>q</mi><mi>n</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}</annotation></semantics></math> be an optimal merge sequence consistent with <math id="S3.p11.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math>, that is, a sequence in which the maximum error of a merge is minimum possible.
Let <math id="S3.p11.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> be the maximum error of a merge in this sequence.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p12" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p12.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p12.1.1" class="ltx_text">We refer to the algorithm from the lemma statement as the greedy algorithm.
Note that the merge sequence <math id="S3.p12.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>n</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}</annotation></semantics></math> it produces is a permutation of <math id="S3.p12.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>q</mi><mi>n</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}</annotation></semantics></math>, as both sequences consist of all merges of <math id="S3.p12.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math>.
It suffices to prove that each merge appended by the algorithm has error at most <math id="S3.p12.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>.
We prove that this property holds for each prefix <math id="S3.p12.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> by induction on <math id="S3.p12.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>k</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k</annotation></semantics></math>.
The case of <math id="S3.p12.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k=0</annotation></semantics></math> is trivial. Consider <math id="S3.p12.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k&gt;0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k&gt;0</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p13" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p13.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p13.1.1" class="ltx_text">Let <math id="S3.p13.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="j\geq 0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">j\geq 0</annotation></semantics></math> be the minimum value such that <math id="S3.p13.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="q_{j+1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>q</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q_{j+1}</annotation></semantics></math> is <em id="S3.p13.1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">not</em> one of <math id="S3.p13.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math>.
This implies that the set of merges <math id="S3.p13.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}\}</annotation></semantics></math> is a superset of <math id="S3.p13.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{j}\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>q</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{j}\}</annotation></semantics></math>.
This in turn implies that the maximum weight in the graph after applying merges <math id="S3.p13.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> is not larger than the maximum weight in the graph after applying <math id="S3.p13.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="q_{1},\ldots,q_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>q</mi><mi>j</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q_{1},\ldots,q_{j}</annotation></semantics></math>.
Hence, the error of the merge <math id="S3.p13.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="q_{j+1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>q</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q_{j+1}</annotation></semantics></math> after applying <math id="S3.p13.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> is at most the error of <math id="S3.p13.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="q_{j+1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>q</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q_{j+1}</annotation></semantics></math> after applying <math id="S3.p13.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="q_{1},\ldots,q_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>q</mi><mi>j</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">q_{1},\ldots,q_{j}</annotation></semantics></math>, which by definition is at most <math id="S3.p13.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>.
Since the greedy algorithm chooses the merge of minimum error, we conclude that the error of <math id="S3.p13.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{k+1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>m</mi><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{k+1}</annotation></semantics></math> is at most <math id="S3.p13.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S3.p14" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p14.1" class="ltx_p">We conclude this section with our main theoretical result, which shows that applying <math id="S3.p14.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges produces a <math id="S3.p14.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate dendrogram.
We stress that the <math id="S3.p14.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges can be applied in <em id="S3.p14.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">any</em> order (as long as they are <math id="S3.p14.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good in that order).
This allows us to remove the greedy aspect of HAC from consideration and achieve better parallelism.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.Thmtheorem6" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Lemma 0.</h6>
<div id="S3.Thmtheorem6.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.Thmtheorem6.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.Thmtheorem6.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Let <math id="S3.Thmtheorem6.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> be a dendrogram of <math id="S3.Thmtheorem6.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> obtained by applying <math id="S3.Thmtheorem6.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges and <math id="S3.Thmtheorem6.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon&gt;0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon&gt;0</annotation></semantics></math>.
Then, <math id="S3.Thmtheorem6.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> is <math id="S3.Thmtheorem6.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S3.4" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S3.p15" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p15.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p15.1.1" class="ltx_text">Let <math id="S3.p15.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{r}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{r}</annotation></semantics></math> be a greedy merge sequence of <math id="S3.p15.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math>.
We use induction on <math id="S3.p15.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>k</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k</annotation></semantics></math> to show that all merges <math id="S3.p15.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> are <math id="S3.p15.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate.
For <math id="S3.p15.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k=0</annotation></semantics></math> the lemma is trivial, so let us assume <math id="S3.p15.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0&lt;k\leq r" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0&lt;k\leq r</annotation></semantics></math>.
Let <math id="S3.p15.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> be the graph obtained by applying the merges <math id="S3.p15.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{k-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{k-1}</annotation></semantics></math>.
Let <math id="S3.p15.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="xy" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">xy</annotation></semantics></math> be the maximum weight edge in <math id="S3.p15.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> (chosen arbitrarily in case of a tie).</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p16" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p16.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p16.1.1" class="ltx_text">We will say that a merge <math id="S3.p16.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>m</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> is <em id="S3.p16.1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">available</em> if it’s one of the merges in the merge tree of <math id="S3.p16.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math>, and <math id="S3.p16.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{k},m^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>m</mi><mo>′</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{k},m^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> is consistent with the merge tree of <math id="S3.p16.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math>.
Our goal is to show that <math id="S3.p16.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> is <math id="S3.p16.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate.
Hence, we need to show that the merge similarity of <math id="S3.p16.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> is at least <math id="S3.p16.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w(xy)/(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w(xy)/(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>.
To that end, it suffices to show that there is an available merge of similarity at least <math id="S3.p16.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w(xy)/(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w(xy)/(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>.
This is because, thanks to the greedy construction, <math id="S3.p16.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> is the available merge of maximum merge similarity.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p17" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p17.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p17.1.1" class="ltx_text">Consider the first merges in the dendrogram <math id="S3.p17.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math>, which involved vertices <math id="S3.p17.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>x</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S3.p17.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="y" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>y</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">y</annotation></semantics></math>.
That is, let <math id="S3.p17.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> be the sibling vertex of <math id="S3.p17.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>x</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x</annotation></semantics></math> in the dendrogram <math id="S3.p17.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S3.p17.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="y^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>y</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">y^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> be the sibling vertex of <math id="S3.p17.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="y" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>y</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">y</annotation></semantics></math> (i.e., <math id="S3.p17.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> is the cluster with which <math id="S3.p17.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>x</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x</annotation></semantics></math> is merged in the dendrogram).</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p18" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p18.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p18.1.1" class="ltx_text">If <math id="S3.p18.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x^{\prime}=y" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^{\prime}=y</annotation></semantics></math>, then also <math id="S3.p18.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="y^{\prime}=x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>y</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">y^{\prime}=x</annotation></semantics></math> and the merge of <math id="S3.p18.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>x</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="S3.p18.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="y" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>y</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">y</annotation></semantics></math> is available.
The weight of this merge is <math id="S3.p18.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w(xy)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w(xy)</annotation></semantics></math>, which is trivially more than <math id="S3.p18.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w(xy)/(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w(xy)/(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>.
This concludes the proof in this case.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p19" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p19.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p19.1.1" class="ltx_text">Now consider the case when <math id="S3.p19.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x^{\prime}\neq y" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>≠</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^{\prime}\neq y</annotation></semantics></math> (which also means that <math id="S3.p19.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="y^{\prime}\neq x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>y</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>≠</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">y^{\prime}\neq x</annotation></semantics></math>).
Without loss of generality, assume that in the merge sequence which produced dendrogram <math id="S3.p19.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math>, the merge of <math id="S3.p19.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="S3.p19.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>x</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x</annotation></semantics></math> happened before the merge of <math id="S3.p19.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="y^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>y</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">y^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="S3.p19.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="y" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>y</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">y</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="Thmclaim1" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_claim">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Claim 1.</h6>
<div id="Thmclaim1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmclaim1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmclaim1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">We have <math id="Thmclaim1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w(xy)/\min(\mathrm{M}(x^{\prime}),\mathrm{M}(x),w(xx^{\prime}))=\\
w(xy)/\mathrm{M}(x^{\prime}\cup x)\leq 1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>min</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>∪</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w(xy)/\min(\mathrm{M}(x^{\prime}),\mathrm{M}(x),w(xx^{\prime}))=\\
w(xy)/\mathrm{M}(x^{\prime}\cup x)\leq 1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S3.p20" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p20.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p20.1.1" class="ltx_text">The proof can be found in the Appendix.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p21" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p21.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p21.1.1" class="ltx_text">Consider the subtree of the merge tree rooted at <math id="S3.p21.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x^{\prime}\cup x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>∪</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^{\prime}\cup x</annotation></semantics></math>.
We will show that that all merges in that subtree have merge similarity at least <math id="S3.p21.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w(xy)/(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w(xy)/(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>.
This would finish the proof, as the subtree clearly contains at least one available merge.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p22" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p22.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S3.p22.1.1" class="ltx_text">Consider any available merge in that subtree and assume that it merges together <math id="S3.p22.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="z_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S3.p22.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="z_{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z_{2}</annotation></semantics></math>, where <math id="S3.p22.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="z_{1},z_{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z_{1},z_{2}</annotation></semantics></math> are both subsets of <math id="S3.p22.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x^{\prime}\cup x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>∪</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^{\prime}\cup x</annotation></semantics></math>.
By the definition of <math id="S3.p22.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathrm{M}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathrm{M}</annotation></semantics></math> and <a href="#Thmclaim1" title="Claim 1. ‣ Proof. ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>, <math id="S3.p22.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w(z_{1}z_{2})\geq\mathrm{M}(x^{\prime}\cup x)\geq w(xy)/(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>∪</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w(z_{1}z_{2})\geq\mathrm{M}(x^{\prime}\cup x)\geq w(xy)/(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>, which completes the proof.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>

</div>

          <span id="alg1.l3.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">while</span> <math id="alg1.l3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="|E|&gt;0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>E</mi><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">|E|&gt;0</annotation></semantics></math> <span id="alg1.l3.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">do</span>

</div>
