<div id="alg1.l8" class="ltx_listingline">
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg1.l8.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">8:</span></span>
            
            
             Apply the merges <math id="alg1.l8.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math> in the graph <math id="alg1.l8.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>
  
</div>
<div id="alg1.l9" class="ltx_listingline">
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg1.l9.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">9:</span></span>
            
            
            Remove from <math id="alg1.l9.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> vertices <math id="alg1.l9.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> s.t. <math id="alg1.l9.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{\max}(v)&lt;t/(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>&lt;</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{\max}(v)&lt;t/(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>

</div>
</div>
</figure>
<figure id="alg2" class="ltx_float ltx_float_algorithm ltx_framed ltx_framed_top">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_float"><span id="alg2.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Algorithm 2</span> </span> <math id="alg2.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math>(<math id="alg2.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G=(V,E,w,\mathrm{M}),A,\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>w</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>M</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G=(V,E,w,\mathrm{M}),A,\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>)</figcaption>
<div id="alg2.4" class="ltx_listing ltx_listing">
<div id="alg2.l1" class="ltx_listingline">
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg2.l1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">1:</span></span>
            
            
          <math id="alg2.l1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{A}=(V,E,w,\mathrm{M})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>A</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>w</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>M</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{A}=(V,E,w,\mathrm{M})</annotation></semantics></math>, <math id="alg2.l1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A\subseteq V" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>⊆</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A\subseteq V</annotation></semantics></math>, <math id="alg2.l1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon&gt;0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon&gt;0</annotation></semantics></math>.

</div>
<div id="alg2.l2" class="ltx_listingline">
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg2.l2.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">2:</span></span>
            
            
          A set of merges of vertices in <math id="alg2.l2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math>

</div>
<div id="alg2.l3" class="ltx_listingline">
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg2.l3.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">3:</span></span>
            
            
          Mark vertices of <math id="alg2.l3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="V\setminus A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>V</mi><mo>∖</mo><mi>A</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">V\setminus A</annotation></semantics></math> as <em id="alg2.l3.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">inactive</em>

</div>
<div id="alg2.l4" class="ltx_listingline">
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg2.l4.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">4:</span></span>
            
            
          <math id="alg2.l4.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="T:=" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>:=</mo><mphantom></mphantom></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T:=</annotation></semantics></math> priority queue with edges of <math id="alg2.l4.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G[A]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G[A]</annotation></semantics></math> keyed by goodness.

</div>
<div id="alg2.l5" class="ltx_listingline">
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg2.l5.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">5:</span></span>
            
            
          <span id="alg2.l5.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">while</span> <math id="alg2.l5.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\min(T)\leq 1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>min</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>T</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\min(T)\leq 1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> <span id="alg2.l5.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">do</span>

</div>
<div id="alg2.l6" class="ltx_listingline">
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg2.l6.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">6:</span></span>
            
            
            <math id="alg2.l6.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="uv:=\textsc{RemoveMin}(T)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo>:=</mo><mrow><mtext class="ltx_font_smallcaps">RemoveMin</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>T</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">uv:=\textsc{RemoveMin}(T)</annotation></semantics></math>

</div>
<div id="alg2.l7" class="ltx_listingline">
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg2.l7.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">7:</span></span>
            
            
            Remove from <math id="alg2.l7.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="T" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>T</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math> all edges incident to <math id="alg2.l7.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> or <math id="alg2.l7.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math>

</div>
<div id="alg2.l8" class="ltx_listingline">
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg2.l8.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">8:</span></span>
            
            
            Merge <math id="alg2.l8.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="alg2.l8.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> in <math id="alg2.l8.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>, and update the weights of all edges incident to <math id="alg2.l8.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u\cup v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>∪</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u\cup v</annotation></semantics></math> in <math id="alg2.l8.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>

</div>
<div id="alg2.l9" class="ltx_listingline">
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg2.l9.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">9:</span></span>
            
            
            Add to <math id="alg2.l9.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="T" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>T</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math> incident edges of <math id="alg2.l9.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u\cup v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>∪</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u\cup v</annotation></semantics></math> that do not have an inactive endpoint

</div>
<div id="alg2.l10" class="ltx_listingline">
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg2.l10.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">10:</span></span>
            
            
          Return all merges made

</div>
</div>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="S4" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">4. </span><math id="S4.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> algorithm</h2>

<div id="S4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p1.1" class="ltx_p">The <math id="S4.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> algorithm is presented as Algorithm <a href="#alg1" title="Algorithm 1 ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>. It takes as input a weighted graph <math id="S4.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>, the accuracy parameter <math id="S4.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ϵ</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> and a weight threshold <math id="S4.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t&gt;0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t&gt;0</annotation></semantics></math>.
Each vertex <math id="S4.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> of <math id="S4.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> is assigned a <math id="S4.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathrm{M}(v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathrm{M}(v)</annotation></semantics></math> value which is initially equal to <math id="S4.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\infty" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\infty</annotation></semantics></math>.
The weight threshold <math id="S4.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math> is used to prune the dendrogram by performing <span id="S4.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">vertex pruning</span>, i.e., stop the algorithm once it performs all merges of sufficiently high similarity.
The effect of this parameter will be discussed later. For now, let us assume that <math id="S4.p1.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t=0</annotation></semantics></math>, which makes line <a href="#alg1.l9" title="In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">9</span></a> redundant.
We analyze the <math id="S4.p1.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t&gt;0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t&gt;0</annotation></semantics></math> case in Section <a href="#S4.SS1" title="4.1. Flattening the Dendrogram ‣ 4. 
        
          TeraHAC
        
       algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4.1</span></a>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p2.1" class="ltx_p">The algorithm runs in a loop. Each iteration of the loop is a <span id="S4.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">round</span>, which merges together some vertices of <math id="S4.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>.
The loop runs until the graph has no edges.
In each round, we first partition the graph <math id="S4.p2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> into clusters.
The choice of the partitioning method affects the running time of the algorithm, but from the correctness point of view, the partitioning can be arbitrary.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p3.1" class="ltx_p">Given a graph <math id="S4.p3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G=(V,E,w)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G=(V,E,w)</annotation></semantics></math> and a subset of its vertices <math id="S4.p3.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math>, we use <math id="S4.p3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math> to denote a graph consisting of all vertices of <math id="S4.p3.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math> and their immediate neighbors, as well as edges that have at least one endpoint in <math id="S4.p3.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math>.
Formally, the vertex set of <math id="S4.p3.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math> is <math id="S4.p3.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C\cup\{x\in V\mid\exists_{y\in C}xy\in E\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>∪</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow><mo fence="true" lspace="0em" rspace="0.167em">∣</mo><mrow><mrow><msub><mo rspace="0.167em">∃</mo><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>∈</mo><mi>E</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C\cup\{x\in V\mid\exists_{y\in C}xy\in E\}</annotation></semantics></math> and the edge set is <math id="S4.p3.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{xy\in E\mid x\in C\vee y\in C\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo>∈</mo><mi>E</mi></mrow><mo fence="true" lspace="0em" rspace="0em">∣</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo>∈</mo><mi>C</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{xy\in E\mid x\in C\vee y\in C\}</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p4.1" class="ltx_p">Once the partitioning is computed, for each cluster <math id="S4.p4.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math> we compute a graph .
Observe that each intra-cluster edge belongs to exactly one graph and each inter-cluster edge belongs to exactly two graphs computed this way.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p5" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p5.1" class="ltx_p">Next, the algorithm runs <math id="S4.p5.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> algorithm on each graph <math id="S4.p5.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math>.
The goal of <math id="S4.p5.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> is to find a longest sequence of merges which are <math id="S4.p5.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-<em id="S4.p5.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">good</em> (see Definition <a href="#S3.Thmtheorem1" title="Definition 0 (Good merge). ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>).
The challenge is that while the algorithm only sees a subgraph <math id="S4.p5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math> of <math id="S4.p5.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>, the merges it makes should be good with respect to the entire graph <math id="S4.p5.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>.
Once we have computed all merges made by all <math id="S4.p5.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> calls, we apply them in the global graph.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p6" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p6.1" class="ltx_p">Let us now describe <math id="S4.p6.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> algorithm in detail.
The algorithm is given a set <math id="S4.p6.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math> of vertices of <math id="S4.p6.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> and a graph <math id="S4.p6.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math>.
The vertices of <math id="S4.p6.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math> in <math id="S4.p6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math> are called <em id="S4.p6.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">active</em>, and the remaining vertices are <em id="S4.p6.1.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">inactive</em>.
The goal of the algorithm is to perform a maximal sequence of good merges of active vertices in <math id="S4.p6.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math>.
We assume that vertices formed by merging active vertices are also active.
We later show that the good merges in <math id="S4.p6.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math> are also good merges in the "global" graph <math id="S4.p6.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p7" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p7.1" class="ltx_p">To decide whether a merge of <math id="S4.p7.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S4.p7.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> is good, we need to know all incident edges of <math id="S4.p7.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S4.p7.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math>.
This is why the graph <math id="S4.p7.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math> contains not only vertices of <math id="S4.p7.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math>, but also their neighbors.
Since each merge can only involve vertices of <math id="S4.p7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math> (i.e., they do not affect the inactive vertices), the merges performed by parallel invocations of <math id="S4.p7.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> affect disjoint sets of vertices.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p8" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p8.1" class="ltx_p">Given an edge <math id="S4.p8.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="uv" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">uv</annotation></semantics></math> of <math id="S4.p8.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math>, where both <math id="S4.p8.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S4.p8.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> are active (that is, not inactive) we define <math id="S4.p8.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathrm{goodness}(uv)=\max(w_{\max}(u),\allowbreak w_{\max}(v))/\mathrm{M}(u\cup v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>goodness</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>max</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>∪</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathrm{goodness}(uv)=\max(w_{\max}(u),\allowbreak w_{\max}(v))/\mathrm{M}(u\cup v)</annotation></semantics></math>.
By Definition <a href="#S3.Thmtheorem1" title="Definition 0 (Good merge). ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>, a merge of <math id="S4.p8.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="S4.p8.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> is <math id="S4.p8.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good iff <math id="S4.p8.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathrm{goodness}(uv)\leq 1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>goodness</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathrm{goodness}(uv)\leq 1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>.<span id="footnote3" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">3</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">3</sup>
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">3</span>
            
            
            
            
            
            
            
          Somewhat counter-intuitively edges of <em id="footnote3.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">lower</em> goodness are better. Renaming <em id="footnote3.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">goodness</em> to <em id="footnote3.4" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">badness</em> would be more intuitive, but would also sound negative.</span></span></span>
At a high level, in each step the algorithm finds the merge of the smallest goodness.
If the goodness is more than <math id="S4.p8.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>, it terminates.
Otherwise, it performs the merge and continues.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p9" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p9.1" class="ltx_p">We give a near-linear time implementation of <math id="S4.p9.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> which performs a nearly-maximal
set of <math id="S4.p9.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges.
More specifically, the algorithm is guaranteed to perform <span id="S4.p9.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">all merges</span> in the subgraph with goodness in the range <math id="S4.p9.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="[1,T)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>T</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">[1,T)</annotation></semantics></math> where <math id="S4.p9.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="T=1+\Theta(\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Θ</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>ϵ</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T=1+\Theta(\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>, and no merges with goodness <math id="S4.p9.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="&gt;1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>&gt;</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">&gt;1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>.

We bound the running time of <math id="S4.p9.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> as follows.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem1" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_theorem">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Theorem 1.</h6>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.Thmtheorem1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.Thmtheorem1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">The running time of <math id="S4.Thmtheorem1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> on a graph containing <math id="S4.Thmtheorem1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>n</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math> vertices and <math id="S4.Thmtheorem1.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>m</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m</annotation></semantics></math> edges is <math id="S4.Thmtheorem1.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O((m+n)\log^{2}n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>log</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O((m+n)\log^{2}n)</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.p10" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p10.1" class="ltx_p">We provide the proof of this result in the Appendix.
Our algorithm is similar to a recent <math id="S4.p10.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate HAC algorithm <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib26" title="" class="ltx_ref">dhulipala2021hierarchical</a>)</cite>, but is significantly more complicated due to needing to maintain not only edge weights as merges are performed, but also the goodness values of edges.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p11" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p11.1" class="ltx_p">Let us now discuss the correctness of the algorithm.
It is easy to see that each merge <math id="S4.p11.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> performs is good (with respect to its input graph), and that it returns once no good merges can be made.
However, it is not obvious whether the merges computed by all <math id="S4.p11.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> calls are also good when applied on the global graph.
In particular, there are two possible issues.
First, each <math id="S4.p11.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> calls only sees a subgraph of the entire graph and computes good merges with respect to that subgraph.
Second, all merges produced by the parallel calls are then applied on the entire graph, but we do not specify in what order they need to be applied.
We address these in the following two lemmas.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem2" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Lemma 0.</h6>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">For some <math id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C\subseteq V" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>⊆</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C\subseteq V</annotation></semantics></math>, consider the graph <math id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math> and let <math id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> be a sequence of merges in <math id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math>, such that (a) the merges are <math id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good with respect to <math id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math> and (b) the merges only involve merging active vertices of <math id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mi>C</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{C}</annotation></semantics></math>.
Then, the merges <math id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> are also <math id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good with respect to <math id="S4.Thmtheorem2.p1.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>G</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem3" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Lemma 0.</h6>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Let <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon\geq 0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon\geq 0</annotation></semantics></math>, and <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G=(V,E,w)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G=(V,E,w)</annotation></semantics></math> be a graph obtained by performing a sequence of <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges.
Consider <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C\subseteq V" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>⊆</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C\subseteq V</annotation></semantics></math>.
Let <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> be a sequence of <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges with respect to <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>, such that each merge involves two vertices of <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math> (or vertices created by merging them).
Moreover, let Let <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,m^{\prime}_{l}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msubsup><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>m</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>′</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,m^{\prime}_{l}</annotation></semantics></math> be a sequence of <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges with respect to <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>, each involving two vertices of <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p1.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="V\setminus C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>V</mi><mo>∖</mo><mi>C</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">V\setminus C</annotation></semantics></math> (or vertices created by merging them).</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p2.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Then, any interleave of these sequences, that is any sequence of length <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k+l" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k+l</annotation></semantics></math> which contains both sequences as subsequences, is a sequence of <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges with respect to <math id="S4.Thmtheorem3.p2.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math></span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.2" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S4.p12" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p12.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p12.1.1" class="ltx_text">Whether a merge of <math id="S4.p12.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S4.p12.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> is good depends only on <math id="S4.p12.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w(uv)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w(uv)</annotation></semantics></math>, <math id="S4.p12.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathrm{M}(u)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathrm{M}(u)</annotation></semantics></math>, <math id="S4.p12.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathrm{M}(v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathrm{M}(v)</annotation></semantics></math>, <math id="S4.p12.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{\max}(u)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{\max}(u)</annotation></semantics></math>, <math id="S4.p12.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{\max}(v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{\max}(v)</annotation></semantics></math>. Among these, only <math id="S4.p12.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{\max}(u)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{\max}(u)</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S4.p12.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{\max}(v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{\max}(v)</annotation></semantics></math> can change due to merges not involving vertices of <math id="S4.p12.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math>.
However, by Lemma <a href="#S3.Thmtheorem2" title="Lemma 0. ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>, these values only decrease as a result of merges.
By Definition <a href="#S3.Thmtheorem1" title="Definition 0 (Good merge). ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>, the lemma follows.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.p13" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p13.1" class="ltx_p">This lets us show the correctness of the <math id="S4.p13.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> algorithm for any partitioning method for the case when <math id="S4.p13.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t=0</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem4" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Lemma 0.</h6>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.Thmtheorem4.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.Thmtheorem4.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Let <math id="S4.Thmtheorem4.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> be a dendrogram computed by Algorithm <a href="#alg1" title="Algorithm 1 ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> for <math id="S4.Thmtheorem4.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t=0</annotation></semantics></math> and any partitioning method used in line <a href="#alg1.l4" title="In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>.
Then, <math id="S4.Thmtheorem4.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> is a <math id="S4.Thmtheorem4.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate dendrogram.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.3" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S4.p14" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p14.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p14.1.1" class="ltx_text">By Lemma <a href="#S3.Thmtheorem6" title="Lemma 0. ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6</span></a> it suffices to show that <math id="S4.p14.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> is obtained by performing a sequence of <math id="S4.p14.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges.
Consider a single iteration of Algorithm <a href="#alg1" title="Algorithm 1 ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>.
Denote by <math id="S4.p14.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> the current graph in the beginning of the iteration.
The iteration begins by computing a clustering <math id="S4.p14.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> of <math id="S4.p14.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>.
Then, it runs <math id="S4.p14.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> separately in each cluster.
Hence, for each cluster <math id="S4.p14.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> we obtain a sequence of <math id="S4.p14.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges with respect to <math id="S4.p14.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>, which we denote by <math id="S4.p14.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>M</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>.
The resulting dendrogram can be updated with each of these sequences of merges in parallel, since each of these sequences affects a disjoint set of vertices of <math id="S4.p14.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p15" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p15.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p15.1.1" class="ltx_text">We now use Lemma <a href="#S4.Thmtheorem4" title="Lemma 0. ‣ 4. 
        
          TeraHAC
        
       algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a> to show that any interleave of the sequences <math id="S4.p15.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M_{1},\ldots,M_{k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M_{1},\ldots,M_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> is a sequence of <math id="S4.p15.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges.
This can be done by using a simple induction.
For <math id="S4.p15.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k=1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k=1</annotation></semantics></math> the claim follows immediately, as the only sequence that can be obtained by interleaving sequences from the set <math id="S4.p15.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{M_{1}\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{M_{1}\}</annotation></semantics></math> is <math id="S4.p15.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> itself.
Now assume that for <math id="S4.p15.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i\geq 1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i\geq 1</annotation></semantics></math> we know that any interleave of sequences <math id="S4.p15.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M_{1},\ldots,M_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M_{1},\ldots,M_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> is consists of <math id="S4.p15.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges.
Consider any interleave <math id="S4.p15.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tilde{M}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi>M</mi><mo>~</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tilde{M}</annotation></semantics></math> of <math id="S4.p15.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M_{1},\ldots,M_{i+1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M_{1},\ldots,M_{i+1}</annotation></semantics></math>.
Clearly <math id="S4.p15.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tilde{M}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi>M</mi><mo>~</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tilde{M}</annotation></semantics></math> can be obtained by interleaving some interleave of <math id="S4.p15.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tilde{M}_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>M</mi><mo>~</mo></mover><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tilde{M}_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> of <math id="S4.p15.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M_{1},\ldots,M_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M_{1},\ldots,M_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="S4.p15.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M_{i+1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M_{i+1}</annotation></semantics></math>.
We now use Lemma <a href="#S4.Thmtheorem4" title="Lemma 0. ‣ 4. 
        
          TeraHAC
        
       algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a> applied to sequences <math id="S4.p15.m15" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tilde{M}_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>M</mi><mo>~</mo></mover><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tilde{M}_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S4.p15.m16" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M_{i+1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M_{i+1}</annotation></semantics></math>.
The former consists of <math id="S4.p15.m17" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges by the inductive assumption and the second one by the correctness of <math id="S4.p15.m18" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math>.
To apply the lemma we set <math id="S4.p15.m19" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C=\bigcup_{j=1}^{i}C_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo rspace="0.111em">=</mo><mrow><msubsup><mo>⋃</mo><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>i</mi></msubsup><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C=\bigcup_{j=1}^{i}C_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>.
Hence, we get that <math id="S4.p15.m20" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tilde{M}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><mi>M</mi><mo>~</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tilde{M}</annotation></semantics></math> consists of <math id="S4.p15.m21" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<section id="S4.SS1" class="ltx_subsection">

            A flat clustering

            <math id="alg3.l3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{C}:=\{\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒞</mi><mo>:=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{C}:=\{\}</annotation></semantics></math>

</div>
<div id="alg3.l4" class="ltx_listingline">
              <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg3.l4.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">4:</span></span>
              
              
            <span id="alg3.l4.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">for</span> <math id="alg3.l4.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="d\in" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>∈</mo><mphantom></mphantom></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">d\in</annotation></semantics></math> set of nodes of <math id="alg3.l4.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> <span id="alg3.l4.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">do</span>

</div>
<div id="alg3.l5" class="ltx_listingline">
              <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg3.l5.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">5:</span></span>
              
              
              <span id="alg3.l5.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">if</span> the linkage similarity of <math id="alg3.l5.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="d\geq t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>≥</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">d\geq t</annotation></semantics></math> and each ancestor of
<math id="alg3.l5.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="d" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics></math> has linkage similarity <math id="alg3.l5.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="&lt;t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">&lt;t</annotation></semantics></math> <span id="alg3.l5.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">then</span>

</div>
<div id="alg3.l6" class="ltx_listingline">
              <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg3.l6.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">6:</span></span>
              
              
               Add the cluster corresponding to <math id="alg3.l6.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="d" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>d</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">d</annotation></semantics></math> to <math id="alg3.l6.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒞</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{C}</annotation></semantics></math>
  
</div>
<div id="alg3.l7" class="ltx_listingline">
              <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline"><span id="alg3.l7.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:80%;">7:</span></span>
              
              
            <span id="alg3.l7.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">return</span> <math id="alg3.l7.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒞</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{C}</annotation></semantics></math>

</div>
</div>
</figure>
<div id="S4.SS1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.SS1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">While <math id="S4.SS1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> computes a dendrogram, numerous clustering applications require the algorithm to compute a <em id="S4.SS1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">flat</em> clustering, that is a partition of the input graph vertices.
A single dendrogram induces multiple flat clusterings of varying scales.
We now show how to <em id="S4.SS1.p1.1.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">flatten</em> the dendrogram, i.e., compute a canonical collection of flat clusterings consistent with it.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.SS1.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.SS1.p2.1" class="ltx_p">Let us assume that each internal node of the dendrogram is assigned the linkage similarity that was used to compute the corresponding cluster.
Let us also assume that the linkage similarity of each leaf node is infinite.
The algorithm that we use for flattening a dendrogram is given as Algorithm <a href="#alg3" title="Algorithm 3 ‣ 4.1. Flattening the Dendrogram ‣ 4. 
        
          TeraHAC
        
       algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>.
Given a threshold <math id="S4.SS1.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math>, it picks each dendrogram node (cluster) which satisfies two conditions: (i) the linkage similarity of the node is at least <math id="S4.SS1.p2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math>, and (ii) the linkage similarities of all ancestor nodes of <math id="S4.SS1.p2.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math> are below <math id="S4.SS1.p2.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math>.
It is easy to see that this way we compute a flat clustering, that is each node is in exactly one cluster.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.SS1.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.SS1.p3.1" class="ltx_p">In a dendrogram returned by an exact HAC algorithm (i.e., a <math id="S4.SS1.p3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate one) the linkage similarites of the nodes along each leaf-to-root path form a nonincreasing sequence.
Hence, flattening a dendrogram using a threshold <math id="S4.SS1.p3.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math> is equivalent to performing exactly the subset of merges described by the dendrogram whose linkage similarities are at least <math id="S4.SS1.p3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.SS1.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.SS1.p4.1" class="ltx_p">This is not necessarily the case for <math id="S4.SS1.p4.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-approximate dendrograms.
However, we can still show that the linkage similarities along each leaf-to-root path are almost monotone, as shown in the following lemma.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem5" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Lemma 0.</h6>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem5.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.Thmtheorem5.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.Thmtheorem5.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Let <math id="S4.Thmtheorem5.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon\geq 0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon\geq 0</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S4.Thmtheorem5.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> be a dendrogram obtained by performing <math id="S4.Thmtheorem5.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges.
Assume we use Algorithm <a href="#alg3" title="Algorithm 3 ‣ 4.1. Flattening the Dendrogram ‣ 4. 
        
          TeraHAC
        
       algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> to flatten <math id="S4.Thmtheorem5.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="D" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>D</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math> using parameter <math id="S4.Thmtheorem5.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math>.
Then, for each returned cluster the minimum linkage similarity of any merge used to create it is at least <math id="S4.Thmtheorem5.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t/(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t/(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.SS1.2" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S4.SS1.p5" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.SS1.p5.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.SS1.p5.1.1" class="ltx_text">Fix a cluster returned by Algorithm <a href="#alg3" title="Algorithm 3 ‣ 4.1. Flattening the Dendrogram ‣ 4. 
        
          TeraHAC
        
       algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>.
If the cluster has size 1, the lemma is trivial.
Otherwise, the cluster is obtained by merging two nodes <math id="S4.SS1.p5.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>x</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S4.SS1.p5.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="y" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>y</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">y</annotation></semantics></math>.
Since the merge is <math id="S4.SS1.p5.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good, at the point when the merge was performed</span></p>
<table id="S4.Ex3" class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table">

<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math id="S4.Ex3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{\max(w_{\max}(x),w_{\max}(y))}{\min(\mathrm{M}(x),\mathrm{M}(y),w(xy))}\leq 1+\epsilon" display="block" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>max</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>y</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>min</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>y</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{\max(w_{\max}(x),w_{\max}(y))}{\min(\mathrm{M}(x),\mathrm{M}(y),w(xy))}\leq 1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p id="S4.SS1.p5.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.SS1.p5.2.1" class="ltx_text">where <math id="S4.SS1.p5.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\min(\mathrm{M}(x),\mathrm{M}(y),w(xy))=\mathrm{M}(x\cup y)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>min</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>y</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∪</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\min(\mathrm{M}(x),\mathrm{M}(y),w(xy))=\mathrm{M}(x\cup y)</annotation></semantics></math> is exactly the minimum merge similarity of any merge used to build the cluster.
Moreover, Algorithm <a href="#alg3" title="Algorithm 3 ‣ 4.1. Flattening the Dendrogram ‣ 4. 
        
          TeraHAC
        
       algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> ensures <math id="S4.SS1.p5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w(xy)\geq t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w(xy)\geq t</annotation></semantics></math>.
We have</span></p>
<table id="S4.EGx1" class="ltx_equationgroup ltx_eqn_align ltx_eqn_table">

<tbody id="S4.Ex4"><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">

<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_eqn_cell"><math id="S4.Ex4.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\displaystyle\geq\max(w_{\max}(x),w_{\max}(y))/(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≥</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>max</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>y</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\geq\max(w_{\max}(x),w_{\max}(y))/(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
