<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p id="S4.SS1.p5.3" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.SS1.p5.3.1" class="ltx_text">∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.SS1.p6" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S4.SS1.p6.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.SS1.p6.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Vertex Pruning Optimization.</span>
We now analyze the vertex pruning optimization (line <a href="#alg1.l9" title="In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">9</span></a> of Algorithm <a href="#alg1" title="Algorithm 1 ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>), which, after each round removes vertices whose highest incident edge weight is below <math id="S4.SS1.p6.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t/(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t/(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>.
We show that vertex pruning with parameter <math id="S4.SS1.p6.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>t</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> does not affect the final result, as long as we flatten the resulting dendrogram using a threshold <math id="S4.SS1.p6.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t\geq t^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>≥</mo><msup><mi>t</mi><mo>′</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t\geq t^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math>.
At the same time, as we show in our empirical evaluation, vertex pruning brings significant performance benefits.</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem6" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_smallcaps ltx_title_theorem">Lemma 0.</h6>
<div id="S4.Thmtheorem6.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.Thmtheorem6.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.Thmtheorem6.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Let <math id="S4.Thmtheorem6.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G=(V,E,w)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G=(V,E,w)</annotation></semantics></math> be a graph and <math id="S4.Thmtheorem6.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon\geq 0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon\geq 0</annotation></semantics></math>.
Consider a run of Algorithm <a href="#alg1" title="Algorithm 1 ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> with a threshold parameter <math id="S4.Thmtheorem6.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>t</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> followed by flattening the resulting dendrogram using a threshold <math id="S4.Thmtheorem6.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math>.
Then, the output of the algorithm is the same for any <math id="S4.Thmtheorem6.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t^{\prime}\in[0,t]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>t</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t^{\prime}\in[0,t]</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>

<div id="lstnumberx30" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx30.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">  </span><span id="lstnumberx30.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">KVTable</span>&lt;<span id="lstnumberx30.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">VertexId</span>,<span id="lstnumberx30.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx30.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">Dendrogram</span>&gt;<span id="lstnumberx30.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx30.7" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">new_nodes</span>;
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx31" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx31.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">  </span><span id="lstnumberx31.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">KVTable</span>&lt;<span id="lstnumberx31.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">VertexId</span>,<span id="lstnumberx31.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx31.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">ClusterId</span>&gt;<span id="lstnumberx31.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx31.7" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">sh_cluster_ids</span>;
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx32" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx32.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">  </span><span id="lstnumberx32.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">KVTable</span>&lt;<span id="lstnumberx32.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">ClusterId</span>,<span id="lstnumberx32.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx32.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">double</span>&gt;<span id="lstnumberx32.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx32.7" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">min_merge</span>;
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx33" class="ltx_listingline">
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx34" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx34.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">  </span>//<span id="lstnumberx34.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx34.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">Run</span><span id="lstnumberx34.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx34.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">SubgraphHAC</span><span id="lstnumberx34.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx34.7" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">within</span><span id="lstnumberx34.8" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx34.9" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">each</span><span id="lstnumberx34.10" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx34.11" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">cluster</span>.
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx35" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx35.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">  </span>(<span id="lstnumberx35.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">new_nodes</span>,<span id="lstnumberx35.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx35.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">sh_cluster_ids</span>,<span id="lstnumberx35.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx35.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">min_merge</span>)<span id="lstnumberx35.7" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span>=
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx36" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx36.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">    </span><span id="lstnumberx36.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">SubgraphHac</span>(<span id="lstnumberx36.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">clusters</span>.<span id="lstnumberx36.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">GroupByKey</span>(),<span id="lstnumberx36.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx36.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">eps</span>);
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx37" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx37.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">  </span><span id="lstnumberx37.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">dendrogram_nodes</span>.<span id="lstnumberx37.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">push_back</span>(<span id="lstnumberx37.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">new_nodes</span>);
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx38" class="ltx_listingline">
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx39" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx39.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">  </span>//<span id="lstnumberx39.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx39.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">Vertex</span><span id="lstnumberx39.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx39.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">pruning</span>:<span id="lstnumberx39.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx39.7" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">remove</span><span id="lstnumberx39.8" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx39.9" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">nodes</span>,<span id="lstnumberx39.10" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx39.11" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">whose</span><span id="lstnumberx39.12" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx39.13" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">max</span><span id="lstnumberx39.14" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx39.15" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">incident</span>
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx40" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx40.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">  </span>//<span id="lstnumberx40.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx40.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">edge</span><span id="lstnumberx40.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx40.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">weight</span><span id="lstnumberx40.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx40.7" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">is</span><span id="lstnumberx40.8" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx40.9" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">below</span><span id="lstnumberx40.10" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx40.11" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">t</span>/(1+<span id="lstnumberx40.12" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">eps</span>).
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx41" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx41.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">  </span><span id="lstnumberx41.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">graph</span><span id="lstnumberx41.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span>=<span id="lstnumberx41.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx41.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">graph</span>.<span id="lstnumberx41.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">Apply</span>(<span id="lstnumberx41.7" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">Prune</span>(<span id="lstnumberx41.8" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">graph</span>,<span id="lstnumberx41.9" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx41.10" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">t</span>/(1+<span id="lstnumberx41.11" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">eps</span>)))
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx42" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx42.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">     </span>//<span id="lstnumberx42.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx42.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">Contract</span><span id="lstnumberx42.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx42.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">each</span><span id="lstnumberx42.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx42.7" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">cluster</span><span id="lstnumberx42.8" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx42.9" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">to</span><span id="lstnumberx42.10" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx42.11" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">a</span><span id="lstnumberx42.12" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx42.13" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">node</span>.
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx43" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx43.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">     </span>.<span id="lstnumberx43.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">Apply</span>(<span id="lstnumberx43.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">Contract</span>(<span id="lstnumberx43.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">graph</span>,<span id="lstnumberx43.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx43.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">sh_cluster_ids</span>))
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx44" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx44.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">     </span>//<span id="lstnumberx44.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx44.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">Join</span><span id="lstnumberx44.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx44.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">the</span><span id="lstnumberx44.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx44.7" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">graph</span><span id="lstnumberx44.8" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx44.9" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">with</span><span id="lstnumberx44.10" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx44.11" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">min_merge</span><span id="lstnumberx44.12" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx44.13" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">on</span>
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx45" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx45.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">     </span>//<span id="lstnumberx45.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">matching</span><span id="lstnumberx45.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx45.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">keys</span>.
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx46" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx46.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">     </span>.<span id="lstnumberx46.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">Apply</span>(<span id="lstnumberx46.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">SetMinMerge</span>(<span id="lstnumberx46.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">min_merge</span>))
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx47" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx47.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">     </span>//<span id="lstnumberx47.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx47.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">Remove</span><span id="lstnumberx47.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx47.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">each</span><span id="lstnumberx47.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx47.7" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">vertex</span><span id="lstnumberx47.8" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx47.9" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">with</span><span id="lstnumberx47.10" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx47.11" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">no</span><span id="lstnumberx47.12" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx47.13" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">edges</span>.
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx48" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx48.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">     </span>.<span id="lstnumberx48.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">Apply</span>(<span id="lstnumberx48.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">RemoveIsolatedVertices</span>());
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx49" class="ltx_listingline">
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx50" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx50.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space">  </span><span id="lstnumberx50.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">num_edges</span><span id="lstnumberx50.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span>=<span id="lstnumberx50.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx50.5" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">NumberOfHeavyEdges</span>(<span id="lstnumberx50.6" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">graph</span>,<span id="lstnumberx50.7" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx50.8" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">t</span>);
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx51" class="ltx_listingline">}
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx52" class="ltx_listingline">
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx53" class="ltx_listingline"><span id="lstnumberx53.1" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">return</span><span id="lstnumberx53.2" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_space"> </span><span id="lstnumberx53.3" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">MergeDendrograms</span>(<span id="lstnumberx53.4" class="ltx_text ltx_lst_identifier">dendrogram_nodes</span>);
</div>
<div id="lstnumberx54" class="ltx_listingline">}
</div>
</div>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure">Figure 5. </span><math id="S4.F5.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi mathsize="0.900em">𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S4.F5.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;"> pseudocode</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<div id="S4.SS1.p7" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.SS1.p7.1" class="ltx_p">We acknowledge that the fact that <math id="S4.SS1.p7.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> requires the pruning optimization (which limits the output to the "bottom" part of the dendrogram) to achieve the best performance is a limitation of the algorithm.
At the same time, this limitation is also present in the existing methods providing similar scalability, that is affinity clustering <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib7" title="" class="ltx_ref">NIPS2017_2e1b24a6</a>)</cite> and SCC <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib40" title="" class="ltx_ref">monath2021scalable</a>)</cite>.
We note that on the datasets we studied in Section <a href="#S6" title="6. Empirical Evaluation ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6</span></a> using this optimization makes very little impact on the clustering quality.</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>
<section id="S5" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">5. </span><math id="S5.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> Implementation</h2>

<div id="S5.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We have implemented <math id="S5.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> in Flume-C++ <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib2" title="" class="ltx_ref">akidau2018streaming</a>)</cite>,
an optimized distributed data-flow system similar to Apache Beam <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib3" title="" class="ltx_ref">beam</a>)</cite>.
A C++-based pseudocode for <math id="S5.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> is given in <a href="#S4.F5" title="In 4.1. Flattening the Dendrogram ‣ 4. 
        
          TeraHAC
        
       algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Fig.</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a>.
A <span id="S5.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">KVTable&lt;K, V&gt;</span> is a distributed collection of key-value pairs, where the keys have type <span id="S5.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">K</span> and values have type <span id="S5.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">V</span> <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib15" title="" class="ltx_ref">35650</a>)</cite>.
In particular, the input graph to <math id="S5.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> is a <span id="S5.p1.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">KVTable&lt;VertexId, Vertex&gt;</span>.
Here, <span id="S5.p1.1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">VertexId</span> is a type used to represent unique ids of vertices and <span id="S5.p1.1.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Vertex</span> represents a vertex and its metadata.
Specifically, each vertex consists of</p>
<ul id="S5.I1" class="ltx_itemize">
<li id="S5.I1.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="S5.I1.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.I1.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">the list of its incident edges, where each edge is represented as a pair of a <span id="S5.I1.i1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">VertexId</span> specifying the edge endpoint and a floating-point number giving the weight,</p>
</div></li>
<li id="S5.I1.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="S5.I1.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.I1.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">the size of the cluster represented by the vertex (initialized to <math id="S5.I1.i2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math> on Line <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>),</p>
</div></li>
<li id="S5.I1.i3" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="S5.I1.i3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.I1.i3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">the minimum merge similarity used to create the cluster represented by the vertex (initialized to <math id="S5.I1.i3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\infty" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\infty</annotation></semantics></math> on Line <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>).</p>
</div></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div id="S5.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.p2.1" class="ltx_p">The result of the <math id="S5.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> algorithm is a dendrogram represented as a <span id="S5.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">KVTable&lt;VertexId, DendrogramNode&gt;</span>. Each node is represented by
<span id="S5.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">DendrogramNode</span>, containing two fields: the id of the parent node in the dendrogram, and the linkage similarity of the merge that created the parent node.
Both fields are unset if the node represents a root in the dendrogram.</p>
</div>
<div id="S5.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.p3.1" class="ltx_p">The algorithm runs in a loop, until the graph has no edges of weight at least <math id="S5.p3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math>.
Each round first partitions the graph (see Line <a href="#alg1.l4" title="In Algorithm 1 ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a> of Algorithm <a href="#alg1" title="Algorithm 1 ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>).
To this end we use the affinity clustering algorithm of Bateni et al. <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib7" title="" class="ltx_ref">NIPS2017_2e1b24a6</a>)</cite>, which works as follows.
First, each vertex marks its highest weight incident edge.
Then, we find connected components spanned by the marked edges, which define the clusters.
Since each cluster computed by affinity clustering is later processed on a single machine, we use a size-constrained version of the algorithm, which ensures that each cluster contains at most 10 million edges <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib27" title="" class="ltx_ref">epasto2021clustering</a>)</cite>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S5.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.p4.1" class="ltx_p">The choice of affinity clustering is motivated by the following heuristic argument.
Intuitively, the goal is to use partitioning in which many edges inducing good merges (<a href="#S3.Thmtheorem1" title="Definition 0 (Good merge). ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Definition</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>) have both endpoints in the same cluster.
From a vertex point of view its highest weight incident edge is most likely to induce a good merge (if we don’t know its neighbors’ metadata), and each such edge is an intra-cluster one in affinity clustering (unless the cluster is split to ensure the size constraint).</p>
</div>
<div id="S5.p5" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S5.p5.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.p5.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">SubgraphHAC.</span>
Once we have found affinity clusters, we aggregate the vertices of each cluster on a single machine and run <math id="S5.p5.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> on each cluster.
This returns three results.
First, we obtain the set of new dendrogram nodes corresponding to the merges made by the <math id="S5.p5.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> algorithm (<span id="S5.p5.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">new_nodes</span>).
Second, <math id="S5.p5.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> computes the clustering (<span id="S5.p5.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">sh_cluster_ids</span>) induced by performing all <math id="S5.p5.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1+\epsilon)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1+\epsilon)</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges made in this round.
This clustering maps the vertex ids of the graph in this round to cluster ids.
Third, for each cluster computed by <math id="S5.p5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math>, it computes the minimum merge similarity used to obtain that cluster.
For a fixed cluster returned by <math id="S5.p5.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math>, the cluster is obtained by merging together some vertices, which correspond to clusters formed in the previous rounds of <math id="S5.p5.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math>.
Hence, to compute the corresponding minimum merge similarity, we take the minimum of the similarities of the merges used to create the cluster in this particular <math id="S5.p5.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> call, as well as the minimum merge similarities stored in the vertices in the cluster.
<math id="S5.p5.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> processes each cluster on a single machine, so all its three return values can be easily computed based on the vertices of the provided cluster, and the merges which were made.</p>
</div>
<figure id="S5.T1" class="ltx_table">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering" style="font-size:80%;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table">Table 1. </span><span id="S5.T1.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:113%;">Graph inputs, including the number of vertices <math id="S5.T1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(n)</annotation></semantics></math>, number of directed edges <math id="S5.T1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(m)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>m</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(m)</annotation></semantics></math>, and the average degree <math id="S5.T1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(m/n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(m/n)</annotation></semantics></math>.
We show the statistics of the largest rMAT graph that we use.</span></figcaption>

run with varying weight threshold <math id="S5.F8.m22" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(t)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(t)</annotation></semantics></math> on OK, TW, and FS.
Higher thresholds use fewer rounds.

</span></figcaption>
</figure></div></div>
</figure>
<div id="S5.p6" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S5.p6.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.p6.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Vertex Pruning and Contraction.</span>
To complete the round, we first apply vertex pruning, and update the graph based on the result of <math id="S5.p6.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math>.
The key operation is to apply all the merges performed by <math id="S5.p6.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> in this round.
We note that this is equivalent to contracting each cluster computed by <math id="S5.p6.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> to a single vertex.
This is what the <span id="S5.p6.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Contract</span> function does.
Assume that the vertex to cluster id mapping is given by a function <math id="S5.p6.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math>.
<span id="S5.p6.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Contract</span> first remaps each vertex id <math id="S5.p6.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>x</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x</annotation></semantics></math> to <math id="S5.p6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C(x)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C(x)</annotation></semantics></math>, both in the keys of the graph <span id="S5.p6.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">KVTable</span> and in the adjacency lists stored in <span id="S5.p6.1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Vertex</span> objects.
This is achieved by two joins of <span id="S5.p6.1.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">graph</span> with <span id="S5.p6.1.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">cluster_ids</span>.
Once all ids are remapped, vertices from the same cluster share the same <span id="S5.p6.1.8" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">KVTable</span> key, and so we group them by key and merge together to obtain the final vertices.
Hence, the vertex ids in the new graph are the cluster ids computed by <math id="S5.p6.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math><span id="footnote4" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">4</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">4</sup>
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">4</span>
            
            
            
            
            
            
            
          This means that we use the same type to represent vertex and cluster ids. However, we decided to make the distinction to improve readability.</span></span></span>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S5.p7" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.p7.1" class="ltx_p">Next, we update the minimum merge similarity metadata, by joining the graph with the respective metadata computed by <math id="S5.p7.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math>.
We finish updating the graph by applying an optimization: each vertex with no outgoing edges is removed, as it would clearly not participate in any merge anymore.</p>
</div>
<div id="S5.p8" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.p8.1" class="ltx_p">Our implementation is optimized to minimize the number of joins/shuffle steps.
In particular, while a standalone implementation of <span id="S5.p8.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Prune</span> and <span id="S5.p8.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">SetMinMerge</span> would require a join/shuffle, in the implementation we actually extended the joins performed in <span id="S5.p8.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Contract</span> to perform these operations.</p>
</div>
<div id="S5.p9" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S5.p9.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.p9.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Shared-Memory Implementation.</span>
To enable reproducibility, we also provide a shared-memory implementation of <math id="S5.p9.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math><span id="footnote5" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">5</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">5</sup>
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">5</span>
            
            
            
            
            
            
            
          <a target="_blank" href="https://github.com/google/graph-mining/tree/main/in_memory/clustering/hac" title="" class="ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter">https://github.com/google/graph-mining/tree/main/in_memory/clustering/hac</a></span></span></span>
Our shared-memory implementation of <math id="S5.p9.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> is written in the same parallel framework as <math id="S5.p9.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{ParHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖯𝖺𝗋𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{ParHAC}</annotation></semantics></math>, using the Parlay libraries for parallel primitives <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib10" title="" class="ltx_ref">blellochparlay</a>)</cite> and the CPAM system for representing dynamic graphs <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib24" title="" class="ltx_ref">cpam</a>)</cite>.
We provide (1) a faithful implementation of the size-constrained affinity algorithm <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib27" title="" class="ltx_ref">epasto2021clustering</a>)</cite> used in our distributed implementation, (2) an efficient implementation of <math id="S5.p9.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> that runs in <math id="S5.p9.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(m\log^{2}n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>log</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(m\log^{2}n)</annotation></semantics></math> time, and (3) an efficient implementation of weighted graph contraction to maintain the edge weights of the contracted graph after each round of partitioning and <math id="S5.p9.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝗎𝖻𝗀𝗋𝖺𝗉𝗁𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SubgraphHAC}</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S5.p10" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.p10.1" class="ltx_p">Although optimizing the running time of approximate HAC in the shared-memory setting was not the main goal of this paper, we found that our implementation of <math id="S5.p10.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> actually achieves consistent speedups over <math id="S5.p10.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{ParHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖯𝖺𝗋𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{ParHAC}</annotation></semantics></math>, even after optimizing <math id="S5.p10.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{ParHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖯𝖺𝗋𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{ParHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> to apply the vertex pruning optimizations used in <math id="S5.p10.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> (see Section <a href="#S6.SS2" title="6.2. Scalability ‣ 6. Empirical Evaluation ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6.2</span></a>).</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S6" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">6. </span>Empirical Evaluation</h2>

<div id="S6.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S6.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Graph Data.</span>
We list information about graphs used in our experiments in
Table <a href="#S5.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ 5. 
        
          TeraHAC
        
       Implementation ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>.
<em id="S6.p1.1.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic">com-DBLP (DB)</em> is a co-authorship network sourced from the
DBLP computer science bibliography.
<span id="footnote6" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">6</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">6</sup>
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">6</span>
            
            
            
            
            
            
            
          Source: <a target="_blank" href="https://snap.stanford.edu/data/com-DBLP.html" title="" class="ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter">https://snap.stanford.edu/data/com-DBLP.html</a>.</span></span></span>
<em id="S6.p1.1.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic">YouTube (YT)</em> is a social-network formed by
user-defined groups on the YouTube site.
<span id="footnote7" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">7</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">7</sup>
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">7</span>

<em id="S6.p1.1.4" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic">LiveJournal (LJ)</em> is a directed graph of the social network.
<span id="footnote8" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">8</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">8</sup>
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">8</span>
            
            
            
            
            
            
            
          Source: <a target="_blank" href="https://snap.stanford.edu/data/soc-LiveJournal1.html" title="" class="ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter">https://snap.stanford.edu/data/soc-LiveJournal1.html</a>.</span></span></span>
<em id="S6.p1.1.5" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic">com-Orkut (OK)</em> is an undirected
graph of the Orkut social network.
<em id="S6.p1.1.6" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic">Friendster (FS)</em> is an
undirected graph describing friendships from a gaming network.
Both graphs are sourced from the SNAP dataset <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib37" title="" class="ltx_ref">leskovec2014snap</a>)</cite>.<span id="footnote9" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">9</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">9</sup>
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">9</span>
            
            
            
            
            
            
            
          Source: <a target="_blank" href="https://snap.stanford.edu/data/" title="" class="ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter">https://snap.stanford.edu/data/</a>.</span></span></span>
<em id="S6.p1.1.7" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic">Twitter (TW)</em> is a directed graph of the Twitter network, where
edges represent the follower
relationship <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib35" title="" class="ltx_ref">kwak2010twitter</a>)</cite>.
<span id="footnote10" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">10</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">10</sup>
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">10</span>
            
            
            
            
            
            
            
          Source: <a target="_blank" href="http://law.di.unimi.it/webdata/twitter-2010/" title="" class="ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter">http://law.di.unimi.it/webdata/twitter-2010/</a>.</span></span></span>
<em id="S6.p1.1.8" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic">ClueWeb (CW)</em> is a web graph from the Lemur project at CMU <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib12" title="" class="ltx_ref">boldi2004webgraph</a>)</cite>.
<span id="footnote11" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">11</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">11</sup>
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">11</span>
            
            
            
            
            
            
            
          Source: <a target="_blank" href="https://law.di.unimi.it/webdata/clueweb12/" title="" class="ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter">https://law.di.unimi.it/webdata/clueweb12/</a>.</span></span></span>
<em id="S6.p1.1.9" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_bold ltx_font_italic">Hyperlink (HL)</em> is a hyperlink graph obtained from the
WebDataCommons dataset where nodes represent web pages <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib39" title="" class="ltx_ref">meusel15hyperlink</a>)</cite>.
<span id="footnote12" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">12</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">12</sup>
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">12</span>
            
            
            
            
            
            
            
          Source: <a target="_blank" href="http://webdatacommons.org/hyperlinkgraph/" title="" class="ltx_ref ltx_url ltx_font_typewriter">http://webdatacommons.org/hyperlinkgraph/</a>.</span></span></span>
The Web-Query graph consists of vertices representing 31 billion web queries, with
8.6 trillion edge weights corresponding to similarities between the queries (see Section <a href="#S6.SS3" title="6.3. Large Scale Clustering of Web Queries ‣ 6. Empirical Evaluation ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6.3</span></a> for details).
Lastly, we run a set of scaling experiments on synthetic rMAT graphs <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib14" title="" class="ltx_ref">chakrabarti2004r</a>)</cite>,
which are constructed using the parameters <math id="S6.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="a=0.6,b=c=0.15,d=0.1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.6</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.15</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">a=0.6,b=c=0.15,d=0.1</annotation></semantics></math>.
The rMAT parameters were chosen to produce real-world-like graphs, following <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib58" title="" class="ltx_ref">wassington2022bias</a>)</cite>.
rMAT-<math id="S6.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="X" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>X</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">X</annotation></semantics></math> denotes an RMAT graph with <math id="S6.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2^{X}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mn>2</mn><mi>X</mi></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2^{X}</annotation></semantics></math> nodes and <math id="S6.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="50\cdot 2^{X}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>50</mn><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mi>X</mi></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">50\cdot 2^{X}</annotation></semantics></math> undirected edges (before removing duplicate edges).</p>
</div>
<div id="S6.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.p2.1" class="ltx_p">We note that the large real-world graphs that we study are
not weighted, and so we set the similarity of an edge <math id="S6.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(u,v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(u,v)</annotation></semantics></math>
to <math id="S6.p2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{1}{\log(deg(u)+deg(v))}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>e</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>g</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>e</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>g</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{1}{\log(deg(u)+deg(v))}</annotation></semantics></math>. We use this weighting
scheme since it promotes merging low-degree vertices over high-degree
vertices (unless there are sufficiently many edges); the same scheme
was also used by recent work on the topic <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib26" title="" class="ltx_ref">dhulipala2021hierarchical</a>; <a href="#bib.bib25" title="" class="ltx_ref">parhac</a>)</cite>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S6.p3" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S6.p3.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Building Similarity Graphs from Pointsets.</span>
Our quality experiments run on graphs built from a pointset by
computing the approximate nearest neighbors (ANN) of
each point, and converting the distances to similarities.
We convert distances to similarities
using the formula <math id="S6.p3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{sim}(u,v)=\frac{1}{1+\mathsf{dist}(u,v)}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>𝗌𝗂𝗆</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>𝖽𝗂𝗌𝗍</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfrac></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{sim}(u,v)=\frac{1}{1+\mathsf{dist}(u,v)}</annotation></semantics></math>.
We then scale the similarities by dividing each similarity by the maximum similarity to ensure that the maximum similarity is <math id="S6.p3.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>.
The same method of similarity graph construction was also used
in a recent paper on shared-memory parallel HAC <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib25" title="" class="ltx_ref">parhac</a>)</cite>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S6.p4" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S6.p4.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.p4.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Machine Configuration.</span>
Our experiments are performed in a shared datacenter managed using Borg <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib57" title="" class="ltx_ref">49065</a>)</cite>.
Hence, our jobs compete for resources with
other jobs, and run alongside other jobs on the same machines.
We use a maximum of 100 machines to solve all problems
and a maximum of 800 hyper-threads across all machines,
unless mentioned otherwise.
For details of the network topology, see <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib50" title="" class="ltx_ref">poutievski2022jupiter</a>)</cite>.
We ran each experiment three times, and find that the difference in running times across different trials was not significant (within 10%), unless specified otherwise.</p>
</div>
<div id="S6.p5" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S6.p5.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.p5.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Algorithms.</span>
We compare <math id="S6.p5.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> with several HAC baselines.
We use <math id="S6.p5.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math><math id="S6.p5.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="{}_{\epsilon=x}^{t=y}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mmultiscripts><mphantom></mphantom><mprescripts></mprescripts><mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow></mrow></mmultiscripts><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{}_{\epsilon=x}^{t=y}</annotation></semantics></math> to denote <math id="S6.p5.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> run
with parameters <math id="S6.p5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon=x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon=x</annotation></semantics></math> and weight threshold <math id="S6.p5.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t=y" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t=y</annotation></semantics></math>. When not
specified, <math id="S6.p5.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> denotes running the algorithm with <math id="S6.p5.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon=0.1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon=0.1</annotation></semantics></math>
and <math id="S6.p5.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t=0.01" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.01</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t=0.01</annotation></semantics></math>, which are default parameter settings that we empirically
find work well across a wide range of datasets.
Importantly, setting <math id="S6.p5.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon=0</annotation></semantics></math> yields the exact HAC algorithm.
We also compare our algorithm with an optimized distributed
implementation (written in the same framework) of the recently developed
SCC algorithm <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib40" title="" class="ltx_ref">monath2021scalable</a>)</cite>, which we refer to as <math id="S6.p5.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SCC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝖢𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SCC}</annotation></semantics></math>.
The SCC algorithm is parameterized by <math id="S6.p5.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>r</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r</annotation></semantics></math>, the number of rounds used (denoted <math id="S6.p5.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SCC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝖢𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SCC}</annotation></semantics></math>-r).
Increasing <math id="S6.p5.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>r</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r</annotation></semantics></math> was observed to increase the quality of the algorithm <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_citep">(<a href="#bib.bib40" title="" class="ltx_ref">monath2021scalable</a>)</cite>.
We evaluate a lower-quality (<math id="S6.p5.m15" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SCC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝖢𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SCC}</annotation></semantics></math>-5), medium-quality (<math id="S6.p5.m16" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SCC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝖢𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SCC}</annotation></semantics></math>-25) and
high-quality (<math id="S6.p5.m17" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SCC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝖢𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SCC}</annotation></semantics></math>-100) setting.
We also (indirectly) compare our algorithm with <math id="S6.p5.m18" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{RAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖱𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{RAC}</annotation></semantics></math> (e.g., in Fig. <a href="#S1.F2" title="Figure 2 ‣ 1.2. Further Related Work ‣ 1. Introduction ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>, discussed
in Section <a href="#S1" title="1. Introduction ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>).
Although we did not perform a running time comparison between <math id="S6.p5.m19" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.p5.m20" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{RAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖱𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{RAC}</annotation></semantics></math> in this paper, <math id="S6.p5.m21" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> using <math id="S6.p5.m22" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon=0</annotation></semantics></math> should be strictly superior to <math id="S6.p5.m23" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{RAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖱𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{RAC}</annotation></semantics></math> in practice and can be viewed essentially as an optimized version of the <math id="S6.p5.m24" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{RAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖱𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{RAC}</annotation></semantics></math> algorithm. This is because for <math id="S6.p5.m25" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon=0</annotation></semantics></math> <math id="S6.p5.m26" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> performs <math id="S6.p5.m27" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>-good merges, which are exactly the merges that the RAC algorithm can perform (see Observation <a href="#Thmobservation1" title="Observation 1. ‣ 3. Approximate Nearest-Neighbor Chain Algorithm ‣ TeraHAC: Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering of Trillion-Edge Graphs" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>).
However, contrary to RAC, <math id="S6.p5.m28" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> may perform multiple merges involving the same vertex in a single round.</p>
</div>
<figure id="S6.T2" class="ltx_table">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering" style="font-size:90%;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table">Table 2. </span>Adjusted Rand-Index (ARI), Normalized Mutual
Information (NMI), Dendrogram Purity, and Dasgupta cost
of <math id="S6.T2.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math> (columns 2–5) versus <math id="S6.T2.m15" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SCC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝖢𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SCC}</annotation></semantics></math>-5, <math id="S6.T2.m16" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SCC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝖢𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SCC}</annotation></semantics></math>-25, <math id="S6.T2.m17" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{SCC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖲𝖢𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{SCC}</annotation></semantics></math>-100 (columns 6–8),
the HAC implementation of average linkage from SciPy (column 9), and the DBSCAN implementation from SciPy (column 10).
Note that <math id="S6.T2.m18" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math><math id="S6.T2.m19" class="ltx_Math" alttext="{}_{\epsilon=0}^{t=0}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mmultiscripts><mphantom></mphantom><mprescripts></mprescripts><mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow></mrow></mmultiscripts><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{}_{\epsilon=0}^{t=0}</annotation></semantics></math> is the same as the <math id="S6.T2.m20" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{RAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖱𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{RAC}</annotation></semantics></math> algorithm.
For DBSCAN, we perform a grid search for <math id="S6.T2.m21" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon\in[0.01,10000]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mn>0.01</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>10000</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon\in[0.01,10000]</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.T2.m22" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{minpts}\in[2,128]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝗆𝗂𝗇𝗉𝗍𝗌</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>2,128</mn><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{minpts}\in[2,128]</annotation></semantics></math> and use the best scores that we obtain.
All graph-based implementations are run
over the similarity graph constructed from an approximate <math id="S6.T2.m23" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>k</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k</annotation></semantics></math>-NN graph with <math id="S6.T2.m24" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k=25" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k=25</annotation></semantics></math>.
The Dasgupta cost is computed over the complete similarity graph generated from
the all-pairs distance graph, and thus takes into account all pairwise similarities.
In the case of Dasgupta cost, lower values are better.
For the remaining measures, higher values are better.
We display the best quality score for each graph in green and underlined.
We show the relative improvement of each score over the score for <math id="S6.T2.m25" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{TeraHAC}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝖳𝖾𝗋𝖺𝖧𝖠𝖢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{TeraHAC}</annotation></semantics></math><math id="S6.T2.m26" class="ltx_Math" alttext="{}_{\epsilon=0}^{t=0}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mmultiscripts><mphantom></mphantom><mprescripts></mprescripts><mrow></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow></mrow></mmultiscripts><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{}_{\epsilon=0}^{t=0}</annotation></semantics></math> in parenthesis after each score.</figcaption>

<span id="S6.T2.15.2.1.1.1" class="ltx_tabular ltx_align_middle">
<span id="S6.T2.15.2.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_tr">
<span id="S6.T2.15.2.1.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_td ltx_nopad_r ltx_align_center" style="padding-left:2.0pt;padding-right:2.0pt;">
<span id="S6.T2.15.2.1.1.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_transformed_outer" style="width:6.2pt;height:17.1pt;vertical-align:-0.0pt;"><span class="ltx_transformed_inner" style="width:17.1pt;transform:translate(-5.5pt,-5.5pt) rotate(-90deg) ;">
<span id="S6.T2.15.2.1.1.1.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_p">ARI</span>
</span></span></span></span>
</span></span></td>
<td id="S6.T2.15.2.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_border_t" style="padding-left:2.0pt;padding-right:2.0pt;"><em id="S6.T2.15.2.2.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic" style="font-size:90%;">iris</em></td>

<td id="S6.T2.15.16.7" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center" style="padding-left:2.0pt;padding-right:2.0pt;"><span id="S6.T2.15.16.7.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">–</span></td>
<td id="S6.T2.15.16.8" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_border_r" style="padding-left:2.0pt;padding-right:2.0pt;"><span id="S6.T2.15.16.8.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">–</span></td>
<td id="S6.T2.15.16.9" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center" style="padding-left:2.0pt;padding-right:2.0pt;"><span id="S6.T2.15.16.9.1" class="ltx_text ltx_underline" style="font-size:90%;--ltx-fg-color:#008000;">240476750 (1.01x)</span></td>
<td id="S6.T2.15.16.10" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center" style="padding-left:2.0pt;padding-right:2.0pt;"><span id="S6.T2.15.16.10.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">–</span></td></tr>
